```python
class _Drained(Simulated):
    """A link that queues `script[n]` as the `n`th drain begins: the commands reach the loop at
    the boundary before trial `n - 1`."""

    def __init__(self, script):
        super().__init__()
        self.script, self.drains = dict(script), 0

    def drain(self) -> list:
        self.drains += 1
        for command in self.script.get(self.drains, ()):
            self.queue(command)
        return super().drain()


def _near_far(**block) -> Block:
    return Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("near", {"target_x": 5.0}, target=4),
                                       Condition("far", {"target_x": 12.0}, target=4)], **block)


def test_a_person_clears_a_trial_from_a_conditions_count_and_it_is_recorded(tmp_path):
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: "there should be some way in the console to manually advance beyond or
    clear a specific trial in the count", recorded. The block owes `near` one trial fewer; the
    control row and the run's end row say who and where; a clear of a condition the block does not
    have is refused with the sentence, and changes nothing."""
    link = _Drained({3: [ClearOwed(condition="near", by=Box("jake")),
                         ClearOwed(condition="nowhere", by=Box("jake"))]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()]), link=link)

    session.run()

    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    assert sum(r["condition"] == "near" and r["counting"] != "owed" for r in rows) == 3
    assert sum(r["condition"] == "far" and r["counting"] != "owed" for r in rows) == 4
    (cleared,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "clear"]
    assert (cleared["condition"], cleared["block"], cleared["by"], cleared["trial_index"]) == (
        "near", "b", {"kind": "box", "name": "jake"}, 2)
    (end,) = [row for row in _runs(session) if row["event"] == "end"]
    assert [(s["condition"], s["trials"], s["because"], s["possible_avoidance"], s["by"],
             s["after_trial"]) for s in end["shortfalls"]] == [
        ("near", 1, "cleared", False, {"kind": "box", "name": "jake"}, 1)]
    assert [(name, why) for name, _, why in session.refusals] == [
        ("clear", "block 'b' has no condition 'nowhere', so nothing is cleared")]


def test_a_clear_sent_while_paused_is_applied_at_once(tmp_path):
    """The paused loop drains commands too (`_hold`), and a clear is applied there, before the
    resume: routed beside the pause, outside `Session._command`'s welfare-listed parts."""
    link = _Scripted(script={1: [ClearOwed(condition="near", by=Box("sam"))],
                             2: [Resume(by=Box("sam"))]}, step=1.0)
    link.queue(Pause(by=Box("jake")))
    session, wall = _walled(tmp_path, link, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()])
    link.wall = wall

    session.run()

    kinds = [row["kind"] for row in _controls_rows(session)]
    assert kinds[:3] == ["pause", "clear", "resume"]
    assert session.stop_kind == "completed"


def test_a_trainer_switches_drumming_on_for_the_run_and_every_row_says_so(tmp_path):
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: drumming switched on by the trainer, and recorded: a control row naming
    who switched it and to what, and every trial row from then on drawn while drumming."""
    link = _Drained({1: [Drum(scope="run", by=Box("jake"))]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()]), link=link)

    session.run()

    (switched,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "drum"]
    assert (switched["scope"], switched["was"], switched["now"], switched["by"]) == (
        "run", None, "run", {"kind": "box", "name": "jake"})
    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    assert {row["drumming"] for row in rows} == {"run"}
    assert all(row["drummed"] == (before["outcome"] != "correct")
               for before, row in zip(rows, rows[1:]))
    assert any(row["drummed"] for row in rows), "the seed gives the run a trial that is not correct"


def test_a_person_clears_the_rest_of_a_condition_and_it_is_recorded(tmp_path):
    """The science review's D5 and the controller's ruling: beside *clear one*, *clear the rest of
    this condition*, so a condition the animal refuses is moved past in one command, recorded as a
    clear is: its control row says how many trials it cleared, and the end row's shortfall."""
    link = _Drained({2: [ClearOwed(condition="far", by=Box("jake"), rest=True)]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=[_near_far()]), link=link)

    session.run()

    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    (cleared,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "clear"]
    (end,) = [row for row in _runs(session) if row["event"] == "end"]
    (short,) = end["shortfalls"]
    paid = sum(r["condition"] == "far" and r["counting"] in ("counted", "spent") for r in rows)
    assert (cleared["rest"], cleared["cleared"], cleared["owed"]) == (True, short["trials"], 0)
    assert (short["condition"], short["because"], short["trials"] + paid) == ("far", "cleared", 4)
    assert sum(r["condition"] == "near" and r["counting"] != "owed" for r in rows) == 4


def test_a_drumming_switch_sent_as_a_block_ends_is_for_the_next_block(tmp_path):
    """The engineering review's Minor 10: the loop drains commands before it advances, so a switch
    that arrives as a block's last trial pays was reset by the advance, its row naming the block
    that had ended. It is the next block's, and its row names that block."""
    blocks = [Block(name="A", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=1)], counting=EVERY_TRIAL),
              Block(name="B", conditions=[Condition("b", {}, target=3)])]
    link = _Drained({2: [Drum(scope="block", by=Box("jake"))]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, trials=400, blocks=blocks), link=link)

    session.run()

    (switched,) = [row for row in _controls_rows(session) if row["kind"] == "drum"]
    rows = _trial_rows(session)
    assert (switched["block"], switched["now"]) == ("B", "block")
    assert [row["drumming"] for row in rows if row["block"] == "A"] == [None]
    assert {row["drumming"] for row in rows if row["block"] == "B"} == {"block"}
```

```python
def test_a_cleared_trial_forgives_one_of_the_debt_and_drops_its_waiting_repeat():
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: "manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count".
    Clearing the trial a repeat is waiting for forgives one trial, records who did it, and the
    repeat never comes; a clear of nothing owed, or of no condition of the block, is refused with a
    sentence."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=2), Condition("b", {}, target=2)])
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)
    broke = scheduler.next_trial().name
    scheduler.record(broke, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)

    assert scheduler.clear(broke, by="jake") is None

    assert scheduler.owed(broke) == 1
    assert scheduler.shortfalls == [Shortfall("b", broke, 1, CLEARED, 0, "jake")]
    assert not scheduler.shortfalls[0].possible_avoidance
    draws = []
    while not scheduler.finished:
        scheduler.next_trial()
        draws.append(scheduler.drawn)
        scheduler.record(scheduler.drawn.condition.name, Outcome.CORRECT)
    assert all(draw.repeat_of is None for draw in draws) and len(draws) == 3
    assert scheduler.clear("c") == "block 'b' has no condition 'c', so nothing is cleared"
    assert scheduler.clear(broke) == f"'{broke}' owes nothing in block 'b', so nothing is cleared"


def test_clearing_a_drummed_trial_moves_past_it():
    """"Advance beyond": the drum's trial waiting to come back is dropped with the trial cleared."""
    scheduler = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=2)], drum=True)],
                          seed=1)
    scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.WRONG_TARGET)

    scheduler.clear("a")
    scheduler.next_trial()

    assert not scheduler.drawn.drummed and scheduler.owed("a") == 1


def test_clearing_the_rest_of_a_condition_forgives_all_it_still_owes():
    """The science review's D5: a condition the animal refuses, owing many, is moved past at
    once."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=20),
                                        Condition("b", {}, target=1)])
    scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1)

    assert scheduler.clear("a", by="jake", rest=True) is None

    assert scheduler.owed("a") == 0
    assert scheduler.shortfalls == [Shortfall("b", "a", 20, CLEARED, None, "jake")]
```

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ClearOwed:
    """**Clear one trial of a condition's debt** in the open block, or with `rest` all it still owes
    (the PI, 2026-10-11: "there should be some way in the console to manually advance beyond or
    clear a specific trial in the count"; *the rest*, the engine C2a plan's science review, D5): the
    condition owes one trial fewer, or none, a repeat of it waiting or a drum's trial waiting is
    dropped, and the run's end row records it as a shortfall with who cleared it (engine spec
    §16.6; `scheduler.Scheduler.clear`). `taskd` refuses it, with the sentence, where the block owes
    the condition nothing."""

    KIND: ClassVar[str] = "clear"

    condition: str
    by: Actor
    rest: bool = False


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Drum:
    """**Switch drumming** (the PI, 2026-10-11: drumming, "a training tool ... where a trial is
    repeated until correct", switched on per block or run by the trainer, and recorded): `scope`
    `"block"` or `"run"`, every trial that is not correct coming back at once until it is, or
    `"off"` until the block ends (`scheduler.DRUM_SCOPES`; `Scheduler.drum`)."""

    KIND: ClassVar[str] = "drum"

    scope: str
    by: Actor
```

```python
    def clear(self, condition: str, by: object = None, rest: bool = False) -> str | None:
        """Forgive one trial of `condition`'s debt in this block, or with `rest` all it still owes,
        as a person does from the console (the PI, 2026-10-11: "manually advance beyond or clear a
        specific trial in the count"; *the rest*, the engine C2a plan's science review, D5): a
        repeat of it waiting, or a drum's trial, is dropped, and the clearing is a `Shortfall`
        naming `by`. Returns why nothing was cleared -- the block has no such condition, or it owes
        nothing -- or `None`."""
        if condition not in self._counts:
            return (
                f"block {self.block.name!r} has no condition {condition!r}, so nothing is cleared"
            )
        owed = self.owed(condition)
        if owed == 0:
            return (
                f"{condition!r} owes nothing in block {self.block.name!r}, so nothing is cleared"
            )
        self._forgive(condition, owed if rest else 1, CLEARED, by)
        return None
```

```python
    def _clear(self, command, index: int) -> None:
        """**Clear one trial of a condition's debt, or the rest of it** (`link.ClearOwed`; the PI,
        2026-10-11: "manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count"), between
        trials or while paused: the open block's scheduler forgives it (`Scheduler.clear`), the
        run's end row records the shortfall with who cleared it, and `controls.jsonl` the control,
        how many it cleared and what the condition still owes. **Refused with the sentence**, and
        nothing changed, where the block owes the condition nothing."""
        scheduler = self._scheduler
        said = scheduler.clear(command.condition, by=command.by, rest=command.rest)
        if said is not None:
            self._refuse("clear", command.by, said)
            return
        cleared, owed = scheduler.shortfalls[-1].trials, scheduler.owed(command.condition)
        what = f"the rest, {cleared} trials," if command.rest else "one trial"
        self._control(
            "clear", command.by,
            f"cleared {what} of {command.condition} in block {scheduler.block.name}; it owes "
            f"{owed}",
            index, condition=command.condition, block=scheduler.block.name, rest=command.rest,
            cleared=cleared, owed=owed,
        )

    def _drum(self, command, index: int) -> None:
        """**Switch drumming** (`link.Drum`; the PI, 2026-10-11: "switched on per block or run by
        the trainer", and recorded): the scheduler drums from the next trial (`Scheduler.drum`),
        `controls.jsonl` records the switch with who made it, and every trial row says whether
        drumming was on as it was drawn. Engine build C3's change log takes it over, with its
        reason."""
        scheduler = self._scheduler
        was = scheduler.drumming
        # The block it is for: this one, or the next when this one's end rule is already met.
        block = scheduler.drum(command.scope)
        self._control(
            "drum", command.by, f"drumming {command.scope} in block {block}",
            index, scope=command.scope, block=block, was=was, now=scheduler.drumming,
        )
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run them and see them pass**

Run the three files: `529 passed`. Then the whole suite: **3632 passed**.

- [ ] **Step 5: Prove they can fail**

- `_forgive` without `self._due = [due for due in self._due if due[2] != condition]`: `test_a_cleared_trial_forgives_one_of_the_debt_and_drops_its_waiting_repeat` fails.
- `_forgive` without its two lines dropping a drum's trial: `test_clearing_a_drummed_trial_moves_past_it` fails.
- `clear`'s `if owed == 0:` → `if False:`: `test_a_cleared_trial_forgives_one_of_the_debt_and_drops_its_waiting_repeat` fails.
- `clear`'s `owed if rest else 1` → `1`: `test_clearing_the_rest_of_a_condition_forgives_all_it_still_owes` fails.
- `Session._command` without the `ClearOwed` branch: `test_a_person_clears_a_trial_from_a_conditions_count_and_it_is_recorded` fails (refused as a command the session does not act on).
- `Session._clear`'s `rest=command.rest` → `rest=False`: `test_a_person_clears_the_rest_of_a_condition_and_it_is_recorded` fails.
- `Session._drum` without `scheduler.drum(command.scope)` (its line `block = scheduler.drum(command.scope)` → `block = scheduler.block.name`): `test_a_trainer_switches_drumming_on_for_the_run_and_every_row_says_so` fails.
- `Session._drum`'s `block = scheduler.drum(command.scope)` → `scheduler.drum(command.scope); block = scheduler.block.name` (the switch's row naming the block that ended): `test_a_drumming_switch_sent_as_a_block_ends_is_for_the_next_block` fails.
- `_encode_command`'s `"rest": command.rest}` → `"rest": False}`: `test_clearing_a_trial_and_switching_drumming_survive_the_wire_with_who_sent_them[clear the rest]` fails.
- `_command_from`'s `if not isinstance(rest, bool):` → `if False:`: `test_a_malformed_clear_or_drum_is_refused_where_it_is_decoded[fields2-…]` fails.
- `_command_from`'s `if data.get("scope") not in DRUM_SCOPES:` → `if False:`: `test_a_malformed_clear_or_drum_is_refused_where_it_is_decoded[fields3-…]` fails.

### Task 9: The console shows what is owed, forgiven and drummed, and offers the controls

Engine spec §16.6: the shortfall "recorded, shown and flagged as possible avoidance"; the scope's item 14, "owed for every condition of the open block" (`Telemetry.owed` named only the queued conditions, one under a block drawing with replacement); and the page's controls for Task 8's commands. Telemetry schema 16 (call 13; the next number if the warnings build took 16, the header says). The page's Still-needed pane lists every condition with what it owes and, beside each owing any while a run runs, *clear one*, and *clear the rest* where it owes more than one -- **only where a trial of the open block can be owed** (`Telemetry.clearable`: the engineering review's Minor 3; a plain "run N trials" owes nothing unless drummed, and a clear there would only shorten it); then each shortfall, a repeat limit's flagged as possible avoidance or named for the rig, a clear's with who cleared it; then drumming and the switches that change it. `wlx serve` builds both commands from a page's body by the wire's own function, as it builds the service's.

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/scheduler.py` (`Scheduler.clearable`, before `clear`)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/link.py` (`SCHEMA` 16 and its history line; `ShortfallRow`; `Telemetry.owed`'s comment, `shortfalls`, `drumming`, `clearable`; `Telemetry.of`; `_telemetry_out`; `_telemetry_from`)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/serve.py` (`_SHAPES`; `_WIRE_KINDS`; `parse_command`)
- Modify: `wl_xcon/web.py` (`DRUM_SWITCHES`, `_FORGIVEN`, `_need`; `fragments`; the page's click handler)
- Modify (test): `tests/_frames.py` and `tests/test_cli.py` (each full frame gains the three fields), `tests/test_link.py` (imports; the schema pins move to 16; three new), `tests/test_serve.py` (imports; three cases; one new), `tests/test_web.py` (imports; four new; the escaping test's frame), `tests/test_page_browser.py` (imports; one new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Scheduler.shortfalls`, `Shortfall` (Tasks 7, 8); `Scheduler.drumming` (Task 6); `ClearOwed`, `Drum` (Task 8).
- Produces: `Scheduler.clearable -> bool`; `link.SCHEMA = 16`; `link.ShortfallRow(block, condition, trials, because, possible_avoidance, by, after_trial)`, frozen; `Telemetry.shortfalls: tuple` of them, `Telemetry.drumming: str | None` and `Telemetry.clearable: bool`, all required, after `warnings`; `Telemetry.owed` keyed by every condition of the open block in its order, zero included; `web.DRUM_SWITCHES`; `web._need(frame, view)`; `serve._WIRE_KINDS`. The page's buttons: `data-cmd="clear" data-condition="<name>"` (with `data-rest="1"` for *clear the rest*) and `data-cmd="drum" data-scope="block|run|off"`.

```python
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Schema 16 (engine build C2a): what is still owed, forgiven and drummed
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def test_schema_16_carries_each_shortfall_and_drumming_both_ways():
    """Engine spec §16.6; the PI, 2026-10-11: a cap's shortfall flagged as possible avoidance, a
    cleared one with who cleared it, and whether trials are drummed. A reader of 16 refuses 15."""
    sent = _telemetry(
        shortfalls=(ShortfallRow("b", "near", 3, "cap", True, None, 12),
                    ShortfallRow("b", "far", 1, "cleared", False, Box("jake"), None)),
        drumming="run",
        clearable=False,
    )

    assert decode(encode(sent)) == sent
    with pytest.raises(SchemaMismatch, match="carried schema 15 and this console reads schema 16"):
        decode(encode(replace(sent, schema=15)))


def test_a_frame_names_every_condition_of_the_open_block_and_its_scheduler_s_shortfalls():
    """Engine spec §16.6: "owed" for every condition of the open block, a condition owing nothing
    included -- it named only those queued, one under a block drawing with replacement -- and the
    run's shortfalls and drumming, read from the scheduler."""
    block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("near", {}, target=1),
                                        Condition("far", {}, target=2)], drum=True)
    scheduler = Scheduler(blocks=[block], seed=0)
    scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.CORRECT)
    paid = scheduler.drawn.condition.name
    other = "far" if paid == "near" else "near"
    scheduler.clear(other, by=Box("jake"))

    frame = Telemetry.of(_session_with(delivered_ml=1.0, already_today=None), Tally(), scheduler,
                         index=1)

    assert list(frame.owed) == ["near", "far"]
    assert frame.owed[paid] == {"near": 0, "far": 1}[paid]
    assert frame.owed[other] == {"near": 0, "far": 1}[other]
    assert frame.shortfalls == (ShortfallRow("b", other, 1, "cleared", False, Box("jake"), 0),)
    assert frame.drumming == "block"


def test_a_frame_says_whether_a_trial_of_the_open_block_can_be_cleared():
    """The engineering review's Minor 3: a plain run of N, whose every-trial table owes nothing,
    offers no *clear* -- one would only shorten the run -- until its trials are drummed."""
    flat = Scheduler([Block(name="session", conditions=[Condition("session", {}, target=5)],
                            counting=EVERY_TRIAL)])
    owing = Scheduler([Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("near", {}, target=5)])],
                      counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED))

    def clearable(scheduler) -> bool:
        session = _session_with(delivered_ml=1.0, already_today=None)
        return Telemetry.of(session, Tally(), scheduler, index=1).clearable

    assert (clearable(flat), clearable(owing)) == (False, True)
    flat.drum("run")
    assert clearable(flat)
```

```python
def test_still_needed_offers_to_clear_a_trial_of_each_condition_owing_one_while_a_run_runs():
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: "some way in the console to manually advance beyond or clear a specific
    trial in the count"; *clear the rest* beside it where a condition owes more than one (the
    science review's D5). A condition owing nothing has no button; nothing is offered once the run
    has ended, and away from where controls work each is greyed."""
    need = fragments(frame(owed={"near": 2, "far": 0, "mid": 1}), view())["rt-need"]
    ended = fragments(frame(owed={"near": 2}, stop_kind="completed"), view())["rt-need"]
    lan = fragments(frame(owed={"near": 2}), view(can_write=False))["rt-need"]

    one = '<button type="button" class="btn" data-cmd="clear" data-condition="near">clear one'
    assert one in need
    assert 'data-cmd="clear" data-condition="near" data-rest="1">clear the rest' in need
    assert 'data-condition="far"' not in need
    assert 'data-condition="mid">clear one' in need
    assert 'data-condition="mid" data-rest' not in need
    assert 'data-cmd="clear"' not in ended and 'data-cmd="drum"' not in ended
    assert 'data-cmd="clear" data-condition="near" disabled' in lan


def test_still_needed_offers_no_clear_where_nothing_of_the_block_can_be_owed():
    """The engineering review's Minor 3: a plain "run N trials", whose every trial pays, owes
    nothing to move past, so *clear one* would only shorten it; the counts and the drumming
    switches stay."""
    need = fragments(frame(owed={"session": 30}, clearable=False), view())["rt-need"]

    assert 'data-cmd="clear"' not in need and '<span class="num">30</span>' in need
    assert 'data-cmd="drum"' in need


def test_still_needed_shows_each_shortfall_a_caps_flagged_as_possible_avoidance():
    """Engine spec §16.6: "its shortfall recorded, shown and flagged as possible avoidance"; one
    the rig made is named for the rig and not flagged (the engineering review's M4); a cleared one
    says who cleared it."""
    need = fragments(frame(shortfalls=(
        ShortfallRow("b", "near", 3, "cap", True, None, 12),
        ShortfallRow("b", "mid", 2, "rig", False, None, 15),
        ShortfallRow("b", "far", 1, "cleared", False, Box("jake"), 20),
    )), view())["rt-need"]

    assert ("3 short in b · possible avoidance: its failed repeats in a row reached its "
            "limit") in need
    assert ("2 short in b · its repeat limit was reached with tries the rig lost (a lost tracker "
            "or a fault): not flagged as avoidance") in need
    assert ('1 short in b · cleared by <span class="who-b">jake <span class="nm">(box, unverified)'
            in need)


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("drumming", "said", "offered"),
    [(None, "not drumming", ["block", "run"]), ("block", "drumming this block", ["run", "off"]),
     ("run", "drumming this run", ["block", "off"])],
)
def test_still_needed_says_whether_trials_are_drummed_and_offers_the_other_switches(
        drumming, said, offered):
    """The PI, 2026-10-11: drumming switched on per block or run by the trainer."""
    need = fragments(frame(drumming=drumming), view())["rt-need"]

    assert f'<span class="nm">{said}</span>' in need
    assert re.findall(r'data-cmd="drum" data-scope="(\w+)"', need) == offered
```

```python
def test_a_member_clears_a_trial_and_switches_drumming_and_each_reaches_the_rig_as_theirs(rig):
    """Engine build C2a: *clear one* and *clear the rest* on a condition of the Still-needed pane
    send that condition, and a drumming switch its scope, each as the signed-in member's (the PI,
    2026-10-11; the rest, the science review's D5)."""
    page = rig.browser_page()
    page.click("#signin")
    page.wait_for_selector(SIGNED_IN, timeout=10_000)

    page.click('[data-cmd="clear"][data-condition="ecc 10"]:not([data-rest])')
    rig.wait_for(lambda: rig.dispatch.seen)
    page.click('[data-cmd="clear"][data-condition="ecc 10"][data-rest]')
    rig.wait_for(lambda: len(rig.dispatch.seen) == 2)
    page.click('[data-cmd="drum"][data-scope="run"]')
    rig.wait_for(lambda: len(rig.dispatch.seen) == 3)

    one, rest, drummed = rig.dispatch.seen
    member = "Jake Westerberg"
    assert (type(one), one.condition, one.rest, one.by.name) == (ClearOwed, "ecc 10", False, member)
    assert (type(rest), rest.condition, rest.rest, rest.by.name) == (
        ClearOwed, "ecc 10", True, member)
    assert (type(drummed), drummed.scope, drummed.by.name) == (Drum, "run", member)
```

`wl_xcon/scheduler.py`: insert

```python
    @property
    def clearable(self) -> bool:
        """Whether a trial of the open block can be owed, so a person may clear one from the
        console (the engine C2a plan's engineering review, Minor 3): trials are drummed, or the
        block's table -- or its structure's -- owes some outcome, before its stimulus or after. The
        flat run's every-trial table owes none, so a plain "run N trials" offers no *clear*, where
        one would only shorten the run."""
        if self.drumming:
            return True
        table = self.block.counting if self.block.counting is not None else self.counting
        if table is None:
            return True
        return OWED in {table.of(o, shown) for o in (*Outcome, None) for shown in (True, False)}
```

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ShortfallRow:
    """One shortfall on a frame (engine spec §16.6; `scheduler.Shortfall`): trials of a condition's
    debt forgiven in a block, `because` its repeat limit was reached -- by the animal's failures
    (`possible_avoidance`) or with the rig's among them -- or a person cleared them (`by`), after
    trial `after_trial` of the run, or before its first (`None`)."""

    block: str
    condition: str
    trials: int
    because: str
    possible_avoidance: bool
    by: Actor | None
    after_trial: int | None
```

```python
def _need(frame: Telemetry | None) -> str:
    """Still needed, by condition: `scheduler.owed` as published."""
    if frame is None:
        return _NONE
    if not frame.owed:
        return '<span class="nm">nothing still needed</span>'
    return "".join(
        f'<div class="kv"><span class="mono">{_e(condition)}</span>'
        f'<span class="num">{_e(count)}</span></div>'
        for condition, count in frame.owed.items()
    )
```

with

```python
#: The drumming switches, each scope's label (`scheduler.DRUM_SCOPES`; the PI, 2026-10-11).
DRUM_SWITCHES = (("block", "drum this block"), ("run", "drum this run"), ("off", "stop drumming"))

#: Why a repeat limit forgave a condition's debt, as the pane says it (engine spec §16.6).
_FORGIVEN = {
    "cap": "possible avoidance: its failed repeats in a row reached its limit",
    "rig": ("its repeat limit was reached with tries the rig lost (a lost tracker or a fault): not "
            "flagged as avoidance"),
}


def _need(frame: Telemetry | None, view: View) -> str:
    """Still needed (engine spec §16.6): every condition of the open block with what it still owes
    (`scheduler.owed`); beside each owing any, while a run runs and a trial of the block can be owed
    (`clearable`), *clear one* (the PI, 2026-10-11: "there should be some way in the console to
    manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count") and, owing more than one,
    *clear the rest* (the engine C2a plan's science review, D5); then the run's shortfalls, a
    repeat limit's flagged as possible avoidance or named for the rig, a clear's with who cleared
    it; then, while a run runs, drumming and its switches (the PI, 2026-10-11)."""
    if frame is None:
        return _NONE
    live = frame.phase == "running" and frame.stop_kind is None

    def clears(condition: str, count: int) -> str:
        if not (live and frame.clearable and count):
            return ""
        name, off = _e(condition), _gate(view)
        one = (f'<button type="button" class="btn" data-cmd="clear" data-condition="{name}"{off}>'
               f"clear one</button>")
        rest = (f'<button type="button" class="btn" data-cmd="clear" data-condition="{name}" '
                f'data-rest="1"{off}>clear the rest</button>')
        return one + (rest if count > 1 else "")

    rows = [
        f'<div class="kv"><span class="mono">{_e(condition)}</span>'
        f'<span class="num">{_e(count)}</span>{clears(condition, count)}</div>'
        for condition, count in frame.owed.items()
    ] or ['<span class="nm">nothing still needed</span>']
    for short in frame.shortfalls:
        why = (f"cleared by {_who(short.by)}" if short.because == "cleared"
               else _FORGIVEN.get(short.because, _e(short.because)))
        rows.append(
            f'<div class="kv"><span class="mono">{_e(short.condition)}</span>'
            f'<span class="nm">{_e(short.trials)} short in {_e(short.block)} · {why}</span></div>'
        )
    if live:
        now = {"block": "drumming this block", "run": "drumming this run"}.get(
            frame.drumming, "not drumming")
        switches = "".join(
            f'<button type="button" class="btn" data-cmd="drum" data-scope="{scope}"{_gate(view)}>'
            f"{label}</button>"
            for scope, label in DRUM_SWITCHES
            if scope != (frame.drumming or "off")
        )
        rows.append(f'<div class="kv"><span class="nm">{now}</span>{switches}</div>')
    return "".join(rows)
```

Run the five files: `974 passed`. Then the whole suite: **3648 passed**.

- [ ] **Step 5: Prove they can fail**

- `Telemetry.of`'s `else {c.name: scheduler.owed(c.name) for c in scheduler.block.conditions}` → `else {c: scheduler.owed(c) for c in scheduler.upcoming()}`: `test_a_frame_names_every_condition_of_the_open_block_and_its_scheduler_s_shortfalls` fails.
- `drumming=None if scheduler is None else scheduler.drumming,` → `drumming=None,`: the same test fails.
- `clearable=scheduler is not None and scheduler.clearable,` → `clearable=True,`: `test_a_frame_says_whether_a_trial_of_the_open_block_can_be_cleared` fails; `Scheduler.clearable` without its `if self.drumming: return True`: the same test fails (a drummed plain run offering no clear).
- `_need`'s `if not (live and frame.clearable and count):` → `if not (live and count):`: `test_still_needed_offers_no_clear_where_nothing_of_the_block_can_be_owed` fails.
- `_need`'s `return one + (rest if count > 1 else "")` → `return one + rest`: `test_still_needed_offers_to_clear_a_trial_of_each_condition_owing_one_while_a_run_runs` fails (*clear the rest* for a condition owing one).
- `_need`'s `else _FORGIVEN.get(short.because, _e(short.because)))` → `else _FORGIVEN["cap"])`: `test_still_needed_shows_each_shortfall_a_caps_flagged_as_possible_avoidance` fails (the rig's shortfall flagged).
- `_need`'s `if scope != (frame.drumming or "off")` → `if scope != frame.drumming`: `test_still_needed_says_whether_trials_are_drummed_and_offers_the_other_switches[None-not drumming-offered0]` fails.
- `parse_command`'s `if kind in _WIRE_KINDS:` → `if kind in _SERVICE_KINDS:`: `test_each_command_the_page_sends_parses_to_what_the_rig_is_sent[body12-expected12]` fails.
- `_SHAPES`'s `"clear": frozenset({"condition", "rest"}),` → `"clear": frozenset({"condition"}),`: `test_each_command_the_page_sends_parses_to_what_the_rig_is_sent[body13-expected13]` fails (the page's *rest* refused).
- The page's `if (cmd === "clear") {` → `if (false) {`: `test_a_member_clears_a_trial_and_switches_drumming_and_each_reaches_the_rig_as_theirs` fails (the page posts no condition, `wlx serve` refuses it, nothing reaches the rig); its `rest: button.hasAttribute("data-rest") });` → `rest: false });`: the same test fails (the second clear arrives as *clear one*).

```bash
git add wl_xcon/link.py wl_xcon/serve.py wl_xcon/web.py tests/
git commit -m "Show every condition's debt, each shortfall and drumming, and offer clear and drum (telemetry 16)"
```
Body: §16.6 and the scope's item 14; call 13 (schema 16: what changed, and a reader of 15 refuses it); the page's controls, gated as every control is, *clear one* and *clear the rest* offered only where a trial of the block can be owed (the engineering review's Minor 3); the red an import error by design; `tests/_frames.py` is in `tools/mutation_gate.py`'s `GLOBAL`, so this push's gate escalates to a full sweep (the engineering review's Minor 13); the count; the breaks seen failing.

---

### Task 10: A hold runs its own time, leaving the target early is a target break, and a miss steps the staircase up

Three changes to the reference tasks' behavior, each one an answer, and a load-time warning beside the first.

**A hold in progress is never cut off by a timeout** (the controller's ruling on the science review's O2). The four detection and search tasks raced `Hold(target_hold)` against `After(response_deadline)` in `verify`, both from the state's entry, and the ranges cross (a hold up to 1 s, a deadline from 0.1 s): raising `target_hold` past `response_deadline`, the ordinary way to shape a hold, ended every held target as `NO_RESPONSE` -- a miss, counted and unrewarded, in the detection tasks (the PI's decision 6), spent in `visual_search`. The deadline now bounds the response only, in the state that waits for it (`stim_on`, `array_on`); the hold runs its own time, and `verify`'s bound is a backstop check 4 needs, 3 s: longer than any legal `target_hold`, and no longer than the deadline's own highest, so no trial lasts longer than it could before. **The same defect stood in each task's fixation hold**, which the load-time warning below finds: `hold_fix` raced `Hold(fix_hold)` (up to 2 s) against `After(fix_acquisition)` (from 0.5 s), so a fixation hold set past the acquisition time was cut off as `NO_FIXATION`, and `calibration`'s `hold` did the same with `cal_hold`. Each gets the same kind of backstop, 10 s, `fix_acquisition`'s highest (call 17). At the tasks' starting values nothing changes: every hold is shorter than its old bound.

**A load-time warning, `hold-cut-short`**, for any state whose plain `After` can fire before its `Hold` can complete, within their declared ranges: a warning, accepted in every session kind (the PI's rule of 2026-10-11, warnings over refusals), saying which state, which hold and which bound, and at what settings. A bound naming the hold's own setting is not one. The warnings build's `beyond_range` re-runs the checks with a range widened to a value outside it, so a hold set past its range can raise this warning there too; its test of `fix_hold` at 8 s, whose state's `fix_acquisition` timeout starts at 0.5 s, now expects it beside `outside-range` (call 18).

**Leaving the target before its hold is complete is a target break in both detection tasks** (the PI, 2026-10-13, answering Appendix B item 6, "Accept, target break": "Take the plan's tables, make leaving the target early a target break in the two detection tasks"). It was `WRONG_TARGET`, a distractor-type answer in a task that shows no distractor; now `TARGET_BREAK`, strobing `OUTCOME_TARGET_BREAK` (4120) as `visual_search` does, so the stream says why the trial aborted. Their tables already show a break after the target again (items 5 and 7), and a target break is no answer, so it does not move `adaptive_detection`'s staircase (`next_params` reads correct, wrong and missed answers only; `test_an_aborted_trial_does_not_move_the_staircase` already holds a target break to that). Nothing else in the two tasks keys on `WRONG_TARGET`: their reaction time ends at `verify`, they offer no choice, and the allocation marks a target break as `TRIAL_ABORT` with its reason.

**A miss steps `adaptive_detection`'s staircase up** (the PI, 2026-10-12, "Yes, a miss steps contrast up": "A miss counts as an incorrect answer, so after misses the target gets more visible"). `next_params` moved the staircase on a correct answer and a wrong one only, so with misses the PI's decision 6 made answers, a target below threshold stepped contrast down on corrects and never back up (the science review's O3); TSK-33's default already counted a miss a failure. TSK-34 records his answer (Task 11).

```python
def test_an_after_that_can_beat_a_hold_on_its_state_is_a_warning():
    """The engine C2a plan's science review, O2: `verify` raced `Hold(target_hold)` against
    `After(response_deadline)`, both from the state's entry, and their ranges crossed, so a hold set
    past the deadline was cut off and scored a miss. A warning, accepted in every session kind (the
    PI, 2026-10-11: warnings over refusals); a bound longer than the longest hold, or the hold's own
    setting, is not one."""
    def verify(bound) -> Trial:
        return Trial(
            start="verify",
            params=[Param("target_hold", unit="s", low=0.05, high=1.0),
                    Param("response_deadline", unit="s", low=0.1, high=3.0)],
            windows=[Window("target", at=(0.0, 0.0), radius=2.0, on="fix")],
            states=[State("verify", enter=[Show(FIX)], go=[
                On(Hold("target", P("target_hold")), Outcome.CORRECT),
                On(After(bound), Outcome.NO_RESPONSE),
            ])],
        )

    (cut,) = [f for f in check(verify(P("response_deadline"))) if f.code == "hold-cut-short"]
    assert cut.is_warning and not cut.refuses("recording")
    assert "'verify'" in cut.detail and "NO_RESPONSE" in cut.detail
    assert "as soon as 0.1 s" in cut.detail and "up to 1 s" in cut.detail
    for bound in (3.0, P("target_hold")):
        assert "hold-cut-short" not in codes(verify(bound))
```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ["fixation_detection", "adaptive_detection"])
def test_a_target_held_past_the_response_deadline_is_correct(name):
    """The science review's O2: with `target_hold` set past `response_deadline` -- how a trainer
    shapes a longer hold -- a target held to the end was scored `NO_RESPONSE`, a miss, because the
    deadline ran from `verify`'s entry too. The deadline bounds the response; the hold runs its
    own time. Gaze holds the fixation point, lands on the target at frame 45 and stays: the old
    bound, 0.3 s from `verify`'s entry, ended it at frame 75; the hold's 0.9 s runs past it."""
    trial = _load_task(TASKS / f"{name}.py").trial
    world = Scripted({SaccadeTo("target"): 45},
                     inside={f: "fix" if f <= 40 else "target" for f in range(1, 400)})
    values = {**VALUES, "response_deadline": 0.3, "target_hold": 0.9}

    result = run_trial(trial, world, 0.01, values=values, effects=Recorded())

    assert result.outcome is Outcome.CORRECT and result.frames >= 45 + 90

    """The PI, 2026-10-13, answering the engine C2a plan's Appendix B item 6 ("Accept, target
    break"): in a task that shows no distractor, leaving the target before its hold is complete is
    a criterion not kept, never a wrong answer -- a target break, shown again by its table and
    moving no staircase -- and the task strobes why (`OUTCOME_TARGET_BREAK`, 4120). Gaze lands on
    the target at frame 45 and leaves it at frame 60, before its 0.2 s hold."""
    trial = _load_task(TASKS / f"{name}.py").trial
    world = Scripted({SaccadeTo("target"): 45},
                     inside={f: "fix" if f <= 40 else "target" for f in range(1, 60)})
    effects = Recorded()

    result = run_trial(trial, world, 0.01, values=VALUES, effects=effects)

    assert result.outcome is Outcome.TARGET_BREAK and ("mark", 4120) in effects.log
```

```python
def _holds_cut_short(trial: Trial) -> list[Finding]:
    """A `Hold` that an `After` on the same state can beat, within their declared ranges, is a hold
    cut off in progress: the trial ends on the timeout, scored as whatever it leads to, while the
    animal is still holding (the engine C2a plan's science review, O2: a held target scored a miss
    when `target_hold` was set past `response_deadline`). **A warning, never a refusal** (the PI,
    2026-10-11: warnings over refusals), naming the state, the hold, the bound and their settings.
    Only a plain `After` is compared -- one with a `since` starts later -- and only a setting with
    a declared range, or a number; a bound naming the hold's own setting cannot beat it."""
    ranges = _ranges(trial)

    def span(value) -> "tuple[float, float] | None":
        if isinstance(value, P):
            return ranges.get(value.name)
        return (float(value), float(value)) if _is_number(value) else None

    def said(value) -> str:
        return f"`{value.name}`" if isinstance(value, P) else f"{value:g} s"

    findings: list[Finding] = []
    for state in trial.states:
        holds = [edge for edge in state.go if isinstance(edge.guard, Hold)]
        bounds = [edge for edge in state.go
                  if isinstance(edge.guard, After) and edge.guard.since is None]
        for hold in holds:
            for bound in bounds:
                held, waits = span(hold.guard.seconds), span(bound.guard.seconds)
                if held is None or waits is None or bound.guard.seconds == hold.guard.seconds:
                    continue
                if waits[0] < held[1]:
                    findings.append(Finding("hold-cut-short", (
                        f"state {state.name!r} ends on its {_target_name(bound.to)} timeout after "
                        f"{said(bound.guard.seconds)}, as soon as {waits[0]:g} s, while its "
                        f"{hold.guard.window!r} hold takes {said(hold.guard.seconds)}, up to "
                        f"{held[1]:g} s: an animal still holding is cut off and scored "
                        f"{_target_name(bound.to)}; bound the state by a wait longer than the "
                        f"longest hold"), blocking=False, accepted_in=SESSION_KINDS))
    return findings
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Prove they can fail**

- `check` without `+ _holds_cut_short(trial)`: `test_an_after_that_can_beat_a_hold_on_its_state_is_a_warning` fails.
- `_holds_cut_short`'s `if waits[0] < held[1]:` → `if waits[1] < held[1]:` (the bound at its highest, not its lowest): the same test fails.
- `_holds_cut_short` without `or bound.guard.seconds == hold.guard.seconds`: the same test fails (a bound naming the hold's own setting called one that can beat it).
- `tasks/visual_search.py`'s `On(After(3.0), Outcome.NO_RESPONSE, do=[Mark(4117)]),` → `On(After(P("response_deadline")), Outcome.NO_RESPONSE, do=[Mark(4117)]),`: `test_no_reference_task_cuts_a_hold_short[visual_search]` fails.

add

```markdown

**Counting and repeats, since engine build C2a.** Each trial structure declares its counting table
(`task.Counting`; engine spec §16.1-16.2), naming the state its stimulus appears in: every answer
counted; a fixation break, no fixation, a
lost tracker and a fault owed, shown again; a break after the stimulus appears spent or owed as the
task says, with no default (`check.check_task` refuses a structure without a table); no response a
miss where withholding is an answer; a wrong answer under "until N correct" unpaid, never placed as
a repeat; and a repeat limit, none unless a task sets one, counting failed repeats in a row. A block
may count by a table of its own (`scheduler.Block.counting`). The scheduler places a repeat at a free
random slot within the next k trials, k at least 3, never the very next, unless nothing else can be
drawn (§16.3); drums a block or a run when asked (every trial that is not right back at once until
it is, only its first try judged for a criterion, and a console warning after ten failed tries in a
row); forgives a condition's debt past its limit -- flagged as possible avoidance only when the
animal's own failures reached it -- or when a person clears one trial of it, or the rest, from the
console; and each trial's row records what it did to the count, whether its stimulus appeared, the
trial it repeats and the condition before it; each run's end row records the shortfalls. A trial
that reaches the longest trial, five minutes at the session's rate (`run.LONGEST_TRIAL_S`), is a
missed acquisition, owed, and said on the console. A hold runs its own time: the load-time check
warns where a timeout on its state can cut it short (`hold-cut-short`).
```

`docs/references/decisions.md`: insert

```markdown
### TSK-52 — Drumming: a training tool where a trial that is not right comes straight back until it is, declared by a block or switched on for a block or a run by the trainer; only each trial's first try is judged; a console warning after ten failed tries in a row; recorded
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI ("a training tool ... where a trial is repeated until correct": its own option, the same trial back immediately until correct, switched on per block or run by the trainer, recorded as a change); 2026-10-12, the PI ("Leave drummed repeats out": "Only each trial's first try is judged for the criterion. The repeats are still recorded as what they were."; "After 10 in a row": "A warning on the console after 10 failed tries in a row", drumming going on). That a correct rejection ends a drum is the controller's ruling on the plan's engineering review (I1), engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#16-counting-and-repeats-n13-nr1-nr2) and its note of YYYY-MM-DD; [`wl_xcon/scheduler.py`](../../wl_xcon/scheduler.py) (`Block.drum`, `Scheduler.drum`, `DRUM_WARNING`); [the engine C2a plan](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-engine-c2a.md#the-pis-decisions-it-builds-binding).
- **Basis:** PI decision. An established option: NIMH MonkeyLogic's "repeat immediately" plays "the same condition ... repeatedly until the trial succeeds" [@nimhmonkeylogic2026task] ("On error"), the alternative TSK-30 records the PI rejecting as the default; here it is a trainer's tool, never the default. Leaving the repeats out of the criterion is the PI's; the plan's science review found that judging them let an animal that always picks one side meet a 60% criterion at 50% true accuracy (its D2, a probe).

### TSK-53 — The reference tasks' counting tables, as the PI confirmed them: each naming the state its stimulus appears in; no response a miss in both detection tasks, and leaving the target before its hold a target break; a break after the stimulus owed in the detection tasks, the search training variant and the calibration task, and spent in the search task; no repeat limit in any
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI (no response a miss in both detection tasks); 2026-10-12, the PI (no limit for calibration: "As drafted. The trainer moves past a target with the console's 'clear' button."); 2026-10-13, the PI, answering the engine C2a plan's Appendix B: items 1 and 2, "Approve"; item 3, "Task names it, required" (TSK-29); items 5 to 13, "Accept, target break" -- "Take the plan's tables, make leaving the target early a target break in the two detection tasks, and give visual_search_training no repeat limit." The question said these tables are engine test material, run only in the simulator, and that real tasks' tables are set with him in wl-xtasks.
- **Where:** [`tasks/`](../../tasks/) (each structure's `counting`); [the engine C2a plan's Appendix B](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-engine-c2a.md#appendix-b-for-the-pi-to-confirm); `tests/test_reference_tasks.py`'s `CONFIRMED_COUNTING`.
- **Basis:** PI decision. That a miss is an answer where withholding is one is signal detection theory, as TSK-27 records [@stanislaw1999calculation] (abstract). Spending a break in the pop-out search keeps its trial sequence as drawn: macaque pop-out performance depends on whether the previous trial's features and target position repeat [@bichot2002priming] (abstract), and priming of pop-out is the lab's own measure [@westerberg2020priming] (full text). Each other break rule is the PI's choice for the task, with the plan's reason beside it.

### TSK-54 — With no repeat limit set, a condition is repeated until done; a limit counts failed repeats in a row of one trial, reset when the condition pays; a person may clear one trial of a condition's count, or the rest, from the console, recorded with who did it
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI ("no limit (repeat until done)", and "there should be some way in the console to manually advance beyond or clear a specific trial in the count"); 2026-10-12, the PI ("Tries in a row of one trial": "The condition is moved past after 3 failed repeats in a row, and the count resets once it is done."). *Clear the rest* is the controller's ruling on the plan's science review (D5), and naming a shortfall the rig made rather than flagging it as avoidance its ruling on the engineering review (M4), both engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §16](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#16-counting-and-repeats-n13-nr1-nr2) item 6 and its note of YYYY-MM-DD; [`wl_xcon/task.py`](../../wl_xcon/task.py) (`Counting.cap`); [`wl_xcon/scheduler.py`](../../wl_xcon/scheduler.py) (`Scheduler.clear`, `RIG`); [`wl_xcon/link.py`](../../wl_xcon/link.py) (`ClearOwed`).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published source.

### TSK-55 — A trial that reaches the longest trial, five minutes at the session's rate, is a missed acquisition: recorded as a hang and owed, repeated like a fault, and said on the console
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI ("it isn't a fixation break, it is a missed acquisition"; XC-287); the cap derived from 300 s at the session's rate is the engine spec §6.8's, 2026-10-07. The console's warning is the controller's ruling on the plan's science review (D4), engineering.
- **Where:** [engine spec §6](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md#6-when-n3-nr3) item 8 and its note of YYYY-MM-DD; [`wl_xcon/run.py`](../../wl_xcon/run.py) (`LONGEST_TRIAL_S`); [`wl_xcon/task.py`](../../wl_xcon/task.py) (`HANG_COUNTS_AS`).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published source.

### TSK-56 — A "balanced" block-sequence order balances within each run
- **Decided:** 2026-10-11, the PI.
- **Where:** [the engine C2a plan](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-engine-c2a.md#the-pis-decisions-it-builds-binding), decision 7; built by engine build C2b with the block-sequence policies (engine spec §13.5).
- **Basis:** PI decision; no published source for the run as the unit.

```

`docs/pending-wl-preproc-amendments.md`: insert

```markdown
# OPEN — engine build C2a: each trial's count and each run's shortfalls

Opened YYYY-MM-DD by engine build C2a ([its plan](superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-engine-c2a.md), "Calls taken in this plan", calls 10 and 11), backlog XC-365. Read against wl-preproc's `wl_preproc/events/rigtrials.py` (`_FIELDS`) and `rigruns.py` through the worktree's `wl-preproc` link, 2026-10-11.

## What changed on our side

Each `trials.jsonl` line keeps the six fields wl-preproc requires and gains seven beside them: `counting` (`counted`, `spent`, `owed` or `unpaid`: what the trial did to its condition's count), `shown` (whether its stimulus had appeared), `repeat_of` (the `index` of the trial it shows again), `at_once` (whether it came straight after that one), `previous` (the condition before it in its block), `drummed` (a drum's repeat) and `drumming` (`block`, `run` or null). Each `runs.jsonl` **end** row gains `shortfalls`: a list of `{block, condition, trials, because, possible_avoidance, after_trial, by}`, `because` one of `cap`, `rig` or `cleared`. No new `runs.jsonl` row kind: the shortfalls ride the end row, since wl-preproc reports any row but a start or an end as a problem.

## The ask

**Read them into the published dataset**, so an analysis can leave owed trials out, tell a spent trial from an answer, follow the realized sequence, and see each condition's forgiven debt and whether it was flagged as possible avoidance. Both readers tolerate the fields today (a trial line's extra fields are taken as they are; an end row is read with `.get`), so nothing breaks before this is done.

## What is deliberately not asked for

No new escape, code or marker: nothing here is strobed. No change to the six required trial fields.

---

```

```markdown
appears in, required too, a cued task's cue (the PI, 2026-10-13), whether withholding is an answer,
any outcome's own class, and a repeat limit. Every task wl-xtasks writes declares one per
structure. Information, under item 1; nothing new is asked.
```

- [ ] **Step 7: The sweep.** As C1 did: **CI's per-push gate** sweeps every function of each module a push changes (`--changed-only --shard k/12`); after the last task, one CI run of the branch against `main` is read **shard by shard**, every line a real `N failed`, never the job's color, and a shard's log counts as read only if it ends with the gate's closing line and "Cleaning up orphan processes" (`gh run view --log` drops lines past a 64 KiB one; the per-job API, `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/jobs/<id>/logs`, has them all). The modules: `task`, `check`, `scheduler`, `run`, `taskd`, `record`, `link`, `serve`, `web`, `review` -- none new, so `tools/mutation_gate.py`'s lists stay as they are. Task 9 changes `tests/_frames.py`, which is in `mutation_gate.GLOBAL`, so that push's gate escalates to a full sweep anyway. **The full sweep too**: this branch changes `tasks/`, on which `--changed-only` never escalates, so one `mutation-full` run of the branch (`workflow_dispatch`) is read shard by shard the same way. Each function caught only by `timed out after 300s` or an import error (XC-275's verdicts) is then neutered locally (`tools/mutate.py`'s own `_neuter_source`) with its own test file run alone, and must fail a named test, against a control of the same files unmutated. A survivor gets a test in a fix commit before anything else.

- [ ] **Step 8: Welfare, byte for byte** -- each listed function's source at the branch's base and at its tip, and `Session._command` with its two new branches taken out:

```bash
cd <worktree> && BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD main) && $PY - "$BASE" <<'EOF'
import ast, subprocess, sys

BASE = sys.argv[1]
LISTED = {
    "wl_xcon/preflight.py": ["gate", "out_of_cage"],
    "wl_xcon/service.py": ["Service._open", "Service._end", "Service._unended",
                           "Service._close_stranded", "Service._start", "_unasked", "_folder_name"],
    "wl_xcon/taskd.py": ["OUTSIDE_A_RUN", "Session._ends", "Session._hold",
                         "Session._manual_reward", "Session.set", "Session._schedule"],
    "wl_xcon/link.py": ["_setting"],
    "wl_xcon/cli.py": ["_settle_departure", "_settle_return"],
}
#: The two branches engine build C2a adds to `Session._command`, outside its listed parts (its
#: `held` pass-through and its one `except` line): taken out, the rest must be the base's.
ADDED = '''        if isinstance(command, _link.ClearOwed):
            self._clear(command, index)
            return
        if isinstance(command, _link.Drum):
            self._drum(command, index)
            return
'''


def named(nodes, name):
    for node in nodes:
        if getattr(node, "name", None) == name:
            return node
        if isinstance(node, ast.Assign) and any(getattr(t, "id", None) == name for t in node.targets):
            return node
    raise LookupError(name)


def source(text, dotted):
    node, nodes = None, ast.parse(text).body
    for part in dotted.split("."):
        node = named(nodes, part)
        nodes = getattr(node, "body", [])
    return ast.get_source_segment(text, node)


def base_text(path):
    return subprocess.run(["git", "show", f"{BASE}:{path}"], capture_output=True, text=True,
                          check=True).stdout


for path, names in LISTED.items():
    base, tip = base_text(path), open(path, encoding="utf-8").read()
    for name in names:
        print("same" if source(base, name) == source(tip, name) else "DIFFERS", path, name)
base, tip = base_text("wl_xcon/taskd.py"), open("wl_xcon/taskd.py", encoding="utf-8").read()
command = source(tip, "Session._command")
print("added once" if command.count(ADDED) == 1 else "ADDED NOT FOUND ONCE", "Session._command")
print("same" if command.replace(ADDED, "") == source(base, "Session._command") else "DIFFERS",
      "wl_xcon/taskd.py Session._command, the two added branches taken out")
EOF
git diff --stat "$BASE" -- wl_xcon/bounds.py wl_xcon/welfare.py wl_xcon/marks.py wl_xcon/stranded.py
git show "$BASE":wl_xcon/cli.py | grep -c '_marks.depart(session, departure)'; grep -c '_marks.depart(session, departure)' wl_xcon/cli.py
```

Expected: every line `same` and one `added once`; the `git diff --stat` prints nothing; both counts `1`. If anything differs, it goes to the PI in plain terms before the merge.

The Status table's Load-time checks row gains `undeclared-counting` and `bad-counting` (refusals) and `hold-cut-short` (a warning).

The whole-branch review (most capable model) follows, then its fix wave, then the merge, which the PI decides once he has confirmed Appendix B and approved Appendix A.

## Appendix A: Welfare summary items

**No function on architecture.md's welfare-critical list changes**, and the list is not widened. Task 11 compares each byte for byte at the branch's base and tip: `bounds.py`, `welfare.py`, `marks.py` and `stranded.py` whole; `preflight.gate` and `out_of_cage`; `Service._open`, `_end`, `_unended`, `_close_stranded`, `_start`, `_unasked`, `_folder_name`; `taskd.Session._ends`, `_hold`, `_manual_reward`, `set`, `_schedule` and `OUTSIDE_A_RUN`; `link._setting`; `cli._settle_departure`, `_settle_return` and `main`'s `_marks.depart(session, departure)` line; and `Session._command` with C2a's two branches taken out.

For the PI's numbered summary before C2a merges, three welfare-adjacent behaviors:

1. **Two console commands pass through `Session._command`** (Task 8), part of which is welfare-listed -- its `held` pass-through, which hands every manual reward to `_manual_reward`, and its one `except` line, which makes a malformed setting a refusal. **Neither listed part changes**: the clear (one trial or the rest) and the drumming switch are two `isinstance` branches beside the pause, each handing its command to a method outside the list (`Session._clear`, `_drum`), and Task 11 compares the rest of the function byte for byte. Neither touches a reward, a bounded setting, a clock or the pump. Because `_hold` drains commands through `_command` too, a clear or a switch sent while paused is applied while paused, as a scheduled stop is.
2. **Repeats and drumming can make a block longer** (Tasks 4 and 6): a condition with no limit is repeated until done, and a drummed trial comes back until it is right. Each repeat is an ordinary trial: a correct one pays the same `reward_correct`, through the same path; the out-of-cage limit (`welfare.must_stop`, read between trials and while paused) ends the session whatever a block still owes, and nothing in C2a reads or changes it. A trainer can move past a trial with *clear one*, past a condition with *clear the rest*, or switch drumming off, and **after ten failed tries in a row of one drummed trial the console says so** (the PI's answer of 2026-10-12), so a drum left on is seen. In the two detection tasks a broken target hold is now a target break, shown again where their own table counts a block, where it was a wrong answer that paid (the PI's answer of 2026-10-13); a plain "run N trials" run, every live run until C2b, still pays every trial.
3. **A trial now ends at five minutes at the session's rate** (Task 5), where it ran up to 100,000 frames -- about 417 s at 240 Hz and 833 s at 120 Hz; **below 333 Hz**, where 100,000 frames take 300 s, which covers the lab's 120 and 240 Hz panels, that is shorter than before, and the console now says when a trial reaches it. **The warnings build's `longer-than-declared` warning now names five minutes** (`check.TRIAL_CAP`, from `run.LONGEST_TRIAL_S`): its approved wording, "about 417 s today, 5 minutes once C2a is built", is what C2a makes true. The welfare limits are still checked between trials only, so the longest stretch without a check is shorter than before at those rates; the checks about once a second inside a long trial (engine spec §20, item 4) are the warnings build's XC-373. **The holds' new bounds** (Task 10) make no trial longer than it could be before: each is no longer than the setting that bounded its state.

## Appendix B: For the PI to confirm

**Answered on 2026-10-13, item by item** (your decision 4: each task declares). Each item below is one thing a task's counting table says, with your answer: items 1 and 2, "Approve"; item 3, "Task names it, required"; items 5 to 13, "Accept, target break". Items 4, 7 and 11 also carry your decisions of 2026-10-11 and answers of 2026-10-12. Four words are used throughout: **counted** -- an answer, which pays one of the trials its condition is owed; **used up** -- paid as a presentation, but marked an abort, not an answer (the spec's "spent"); **shown again** -- pays nothing, and the condition comes back within the next few trials, never the very next (the spec's "owed"); **pays nothing** -- neither paid nor shown again, the block's order bringing the condition back as it would anyway (only "run until N correct" uses it, for a wrong answer).

**Four reading rules, for every task**

1. **Your answer, "Approve"**: four answers to "what does this trial do to its condition's count": counted, used up, shown again, or pays nothing. A task's table gives one of them to every way a trial can end, and the review report shows the whole table for each task.
2. **Your answer, "Approve"**: every way a trial can end, and its class in every task unless the task's own line changes it (none of the five reference tasks has one):
   - **Counted** (your decisions of 2026-10-07): correct; an early or late response to the target; a wrong target; an early or late error; a correct rejection; a false alarm.
   - **Shown again** (2026-10-07): a fixation break; no fixation; a lost tracker; a fault.
   - **Used up or shown again, as each task says** (your decision 4, no default -- items 5 to 10): a target break, a catch break, a motion break and a long blink, once the stimulus has appeared; and **looking somewhere that is neither target nor distractor** (`abort`), which is not a break but ends a trial after the stimulus as one does.
   - **No response**: counted, a miss, where the task says withholding is an answer (both detection tasks, your decision 6); otherwise as a break after the stimulus.
   - **The five-minute cap**: item 4.
   - **Under drumming**: everything that is not right -- a correct answer or a correct rejection -- comes straight back and pays nothing, whatever the table says (your decision 2).
3. **Your answer, "Task names it, required"** -- "Each task's counting table names the step where its stimulus appears, and the load check refuses a table without one. A cued task names the cue step. A small extra declaration in every task; the five test tasks name their target or array step." A break before that step -- a long blink during the fixation hold -- is shown again in every task, as a fixation break is, since nothing was presented; a break after it follows items 5 to 10. A table naming no step, or one its structure does not have, is refused at load, saying which structure and why. The five reference tasks name `stim_on` (both detection tasks), `array_on` (both search tasks) and `await_fix` (calibration, whose target is up from the trial's first frame).
4. **The five-minute cap -- your answer** (decision 3): a trial reaching it is recorded as a hang, as today, and counted as a missed acquisition -- shown again, like no fixation; in a plain "run N trials" run, one of the N; and the console says so.

**Each reference task** -- **your answer to items 5 to 13, "Accept, target break"**: "Take the plan's tables, make leaving the target early a target break in the two detection tasks, and give visual_search_training no repeat limit." The question said these tables are engine test material, run only in the simulator, and that real tasks' tables will be set with you in wl-xtasks.

5. **`fixation_detection`**: no response is a miss, **counted as an answer** (your decision 6 -- "a miss (counts, used up)", read as: it counts, and it is never shown again; it is not marked an abort); a break after the target appears is **shown again**. Since item 6, that includes leaving the target before its hold is complete, which is what a trainer shaping the hold wants; on the rig a blink arrives as a lost tracker, shown again in every task.
6. **Both detection tasks: leaving the target before its hold is complete is a target break -- your answer**, as `visual_search` already called it, where it was a wrong answer: the landing is the report and the hold a criterion, and these tasks show no distractor. Shown again by items 5 and 7, strobed as a target break, and, being no answer, it does not move `adaptive_detection`'s staircase (the science review's recommendation).
7. **`adaptive_detection`**: no response is a miss, counted as an answer (your decision 6), and **a miss steps the staircase's contrast up -- your answer** of 2026-10-12 ("A miss counts as an incorrect answer, so after misses the target gets more visible"); a break after the target appears, leaving it before its hold among them (item 6), is **shown again**. Reason: a break leaves no answer, and a one-up two-down staircase classes each trial as a positive or a negative answer, so a break is neither [@levitt1971transformed] (p. 472); shown again, it is presented at the staircase's contrast then, not the one it broke on. With no target-absent trials, the point the staircase converges on (70.7%) is a hit rate, which "could depend on a change in only criterion, only sensitivity, or both" [@luo2015neuronal] (Introduction): fine for training, worth knowing for recording.
8. **`visual_search`**: a break after the array appears -- leaving the target before its hold is complete, no saccade before the deadline, a long blink -- is **used up**: counted as presented, marked an abort, never shown again. Reason: shown again within a few trials, the array would repeat its target's color and position, and macaque pop-out performance depends on whether the previous trial's features and target position repeat [@bichot2002priming] (abstract) -- priming of pop-out, the lab's own measure [@westerberg2020priming] (full text); used up, the trial sequence stays as drawn. Caveat: an animal could skip a condition by not responding and it would never be flagged, since the console shows no count of trials used up per condition.
9. **`visual_search_training`**: a break after the array appears is **shown again**. Reason: a training variant, so each condition is practised as planned; a delayed repeat is a standard training option [@nimhmonkeylogic2026task] ("repeat delayed").
10. **`calibration`**: a break after the target appears is **shown again**. Reason: its target is up from the trial's first frame, and every target must be fixated to be fitted; in practice calibration ends only as a fixation break or no fixation, both shown again under item 2, so this line changes nothing today.
11. **No repeat limit in `fixation_detection`, `adaptive_detection`, `visual_search` or `calibration`** -- your decision 5, and for calibration **your answer** of 2026-10-12 ("No limit": "As drafted. The trainer moves past a target with the console's 'clear' button."): each condition is repeated until done, and *clear one* or *clear the rest* moves past by hand.
12. **No repeat limit in `visual_search_training` -- your answer**, as built: a color or set size the animal refuses returns until done, and a trainer moves past it with *clear one* or *clear the rest*.
13. **No reference task declares drumming**: a trainer switches it on from the console, for a block or a run.
